After the collapse, surviving enclaves consolidated power through adaptation rather than 
bloodline. This was an age of philosophers, monks, and warlords seeking meaning in a 
disordered timeline. 
●​ The Hirogawan Houses emerged as regional stewards preserving discipline and 
memory. 
●​ The Crimson Council reconstituted fragments of the Fire Dominion’s bureaucracy, 
replacing divine right with ritual law. 
●​ The Seigetsu Lineages weaponized relics of the elemental wars, forming the first 
militarized states. 
●​ The Yukigami Clans retreated north, fusing meditation with survivalism and 
developing proto‑techniques of temporal resonance. 
These movements gave rise to the Kanmin cultural sphere, uniting the Eastern Continent 
through shared philosophy of restraint and elemental balance. 
 

3.3 The Kanmin East (Modern Era) 
By 1326 AE, the Eastern Continent remains defined by competing ideologies of duty and 
decay. Each province holds a fragment of the past, its governance reflecting its lineage. 
Hirogawa Province – Neutral province ruled by Lord Ishigane. Governed through 
community councils and guided by the moral influence of the Bonsai Sage. A symbol of 
balance between martial and spiritual life. 
Crimson Dynasty – Centralized monarchy turned oligarchic council. The Crimson Court 
governs through hereditary lords tied to elemental lineage. Their strict hierarchy is enforced 
by spiritual contracts binding service through blood and ink. 
Seigetsu Empire – A remnant of conquest. Its chain of command operates under a 
perpetual war economy; generals hold more power than its nominal emperor. The Seigetsu’s 
collapse birthed the Ninth Wave. 
Yukigami Remnants – Fragmented tribes across the northern tundra. Once guardians of 
elemental ice, they now exist as fractured lineages haunted by memory and myth. Some still 
serve Okashira in secret. 
Relations: 
●​ Hirogawa maintains fragile neutrality, acting as mediator in regional disputes.​
 
●​ Crimson and Seigetsu remain ideological opposites: tradition vs. domination.​
 
●​ Trade routes between Hirogawa and Crimson thrive, though Seigetsu blockades 
restrict northern access.​
 
●​ Yukigami’s territory is considered cursed – unclaimed but observed.​
 
 
 

3.4 The Western Realms (Modern Era) 
The Western Continent fractured into alliances of necessity rather than kinship. Each realm 
expresses a unique interpretation of survival after the Eclipse. 
Eddara – A confederation of sentient animal‑kin united under the Blood Pact, prohibiting 
intelligent predation. Governed by a rotating Council of Elders representing predator, prey, 
and neutral tribes. Spiritual unity replaces nationalism. 
Verdathal Concord – A network of bio‑engineered city‑trees functioning as both dwelling 
and machine. Governed by a collective known as the Photosynod, which merges druidic 
law with living architecture. They see themselves as custodians of planetary regeneration. 
Varthum Highlands – Isolationist scholar‑state ruled by the Chronarchs, beings trapped in 
temporal recursion. Their political debates can last centuries of subjective time. Their export 
of Memory Crystals influences the entire continent. 
Eresh Emirate – Dream‑bound theocracy where divine monarchs rule from sanctuaries of 
sleep. Governance is maintained through Dreambindings—ritual oaths enforced in the 
shared dreamspace. Excavations of pre‑Eclipse machines blur faith with lost technology. 
Galport Republic – Maritime oligarchy led by merchant guilds and former nobility. Each 
major dock functions as an autonomous district under the Harbormaster’s Compact. The 
Republic’s control of trade routes gives it disproportionate power despite internal corruption. 
Relations: 
●​ Eddara and Verdathal share a protective alliance focused on environmental 
preservation.​
 
●​ Varthum trades memory technology to both Eresh and Galport in exchange for 
resources and access.​
 
●​ Galport’s fleets bridge trade between East and West but also enable smuggling to 
Kojimigakure.​
 
●​ Eresh’s isolation fosters religious tension with Verdathal’s scientific ethos.​
 
 
 

The Marella Caravans 
A clandestine confederation of Moray-like aquatic traders led by Manolo Marella, known as 
the Tidebroker. The Marella operate through submerged tunnel networks and trench cities, 
conducting illicit commerce in pre-Eclipse artifacts, minerals, and living technology. 
Society: Decentralized trade families bound by oath and secrecy. Leadership rotates 
through senior caravaneers who maintain hidden markets known as Blackwater Ports. 
Ki Affinity: Water and Shadow. Their mastery of pressure-adapted Ki allows them to 
traverse crushing depths undetected. 
Relations: 
●​ Galport Republic: Hostile rivalry. The Marella undercut Galport’s maritime monopoly 
by smuggling goods beneath naval patrols.​
 
●​ Verdathal & Eddara: Maintain covert trade, exchanging bio-materials and rare 
spores for metal and relics.​
 
●​ Varthum: Limited contact, primarily through information exchange about submerged 
ruins. 
 

3.5 The Island Nations 
Mycomind Covenant – A collective consciousness of fungal entities functioning as one 
intellect across multiple hosts. It interacts with outsiders through symbiotic avatars. The 
Covenant’s diplomacy is philosophical, valuing information and emotion exchange over 
material trade. 
Kojimigakure Isles – A drifting archipelago used as neutral ground by pirates, exiles, and 
smugglers. No formal government exists; instead, loose guilds manage survival and secrecy. 
The Isles serve as both myth and refuge, linking the hidden economies of both continents. 
Relations: 
●​ The Mycomind aids Verdathal and Eddara in ecological matters, offering insight into 
temporal healing.​
 
●​ Kojimigakure maintains unofficial pacts with Galport’s underworld and select Crimson 
exiles.​
 
●​ The Mycomind refuses any contact with the Ninth Wave, labeling them a contagion of 
intent.​
 
 
3.6 Geopolitical Overview (1326 AE) 
●​ Balance of Power: The Eastern Continent’s stability hinges on Hirogawa’s neutrality. 
The Western depends on Galport’s control of sea routes.​
 
●​ Technological Exchange: Verdathal’s living constructs, Eresh’s dream‑machines, 
and Varthum’s Memory Crystals form the triad of Western innovation.​
 
●​ Trade Corridors: Few stable routes remain. Temporal storms still disrupt long 
voyages. The Mycomind’s dream‑paths and Galport’s navigators are vital.​
 
●​ Cultural Faults: The Kanmin East values reflection and lineage; the Western 
Realms value adaptation and progress. Their philosophies clash where trade and 
faith intersect.​
 
 
3.7 Closing Reflection 
"Kingdoms rise not from soil but from memory. When memory fractures, power 
grows like mold upon what once was sacred." – The Bonsai Sage
 

Section 4: Mysticism and the Flow of Energy 
 
4.1 The Universal Current 
"Energy is not possessed; it is borrowed. To live is to flow, to stagnate is to die." 
– The Bonsai Sage 
All living beings in Densetsu partake in the same unseen current: Ki, the vital essence that 
circulates through the Infinitree’s roots, the air, and the soul. Every motion, thought, and 
breath carries a fraction of Ki — a memory of creation’s rhythm. Time itself is regarded as a 
refined stream of Ki, moving not forward but outward, rippling through all existence. 
While scholars debate its origin, the most accepted belief holds that Ki predates the Great 
Eclipse. The Eclipse merely fractured its balance, causing energy to pool unevenly across 
the continents. These anomalies gave rise to the phenomena now classified as Time Rifts, 
Essence Storms, and Spiritual Convergence Zones. 
 
4.2 Nature of Spirits and the Mortal Continuum 
"There are no gods, only beings so attuned to the current that others mistake 
them for the source." – The Bonsai Sage 
In Densetsu, divinity is not a state of origin but of resonance. The so-called gods and spirits 
are elevated life forms, born of collective emotion, concentrated Ki, or natural cycles 
reaching self-awareness. 
Categories of spiritual existence: 
●​ Elemental Spirits – Manifestations of natural processes (fire, water, stone, wind). 
They embody equilibrium rather than morality.​
 
●​ Ancestral Echoes – The lingering Ki patterns of Suhada and other races whose will 
outlasted their bodies.​
 
●​ Constructed Divinities – Artificial deities formed through ritual, cult belief, or 
alchemical experimentation. Some persist as corrupted data within the world’s energy 
lattice.​
 
These entities may influence the physical world, but none possess true omnipotence. They 
age, fade, and reincarnate like all beings, bound to the same current of Ki. 
 
 

4.3 Regional Interpretations of the Flow 
Every culture understands Ki through its own lens, reflecting its geography, values, and 
scars. 
Kanmin East – Ki is discipline, refined through meditation, art, and combat. Spirituality is an 
inward cultivation, a method of harmonizing the inner current with the world’s rhythm. 
Temples often feature flowing water or wind chimes to remind disciples of motion within 
stillness. 
Eddara & Verdathal – Energy is life’s breath. The Eddaran shamans speak of the Wild 
Current, an ecosystem of Ki where all things exchange vitality. Verdathal’s engineers blend 
Ki with organic machines, treating power as a circulatory system — veins of energy feeding 
their living cities. 
Eresh Emirate – Dreams are the subconscious flow of Ki freed from flesh. The Ereshi 
priesthood enters the Dreaming, merging faith and energy manipulation to shape reality 
through collective dreaming. Their rulers govern through Dreambindings – oaths written in 
the fabric of shared subconscious. 
Varthum Highlands – Ki is memory. The Chronarchs record it in crystalline archives, 
treating time as an extractable form of essence. Their research blurs spirituality and physics, 
aiming to stabilize rifts by replaying ancient energetic states. 
Mycomind Covenant – For the psionic network of the Mycomind, Ki is communion. Every 
spore carries ancestral awareness, allowing the Covenant to experience all points of 
existence at once. To them, energy is not worshiped but metabolized. 
Kojimigakure Isles – The Isles’ hermits view Ki as secrecy incarnate — a force that 
conceals and reveals. They practice resonance rituals using mist and sound, believed to 
mask one’s essence from hostile detection. 
 
4.4 Philosophical Divergence and Conflict 
No belief system in Densetsu is objectively true or false; each represents an angle on the 
same current. Yet this diversity also breeds discord. Throughout history, zealotry and cultism 
have twisted the understanding of Ki into tools of domination. 
The Eclipse Cults of the Dusk Era sought to force unity through control of Ki, believing 
themselves the chosen vessels of the current. Their collapse birthed many of the rifts now 
plaguing the continents. Even in modern times, secret sects pursue the weaponization of Ki, 
seeing purity as a path to supremacy. 
"When energy is worshiped instead of understood, it devours both student and 
master." – The Bonsai Sage 
 

4.5 Practical Manifestations of Ki 
While this section focuses on spiritual understanding, Ki’s practical uses pervade every 
discipline: 
●​ Combat Techniques: Martial traditions harness Ki for enhanced strength, speed, 
and elemental projection.​
 
●​ Relic Forging: Ancient blacksmiths and Crimson alchemists bind Ki into weapons, 
armor, and constructs.​
 
●​ Healing and Agriculture: Hirogawa’s monks and farmers channel Ki through rhythm 
and breath to accelerate natural growth.​
 
●​ Temporal Studies: Varthum’s scholars extract and condense Ki to stabilize or 
manipulate rifts.​
 
These arts will be expanded upon in later documentation, but their presence defines every 
civilization’s relationship with life, death, and power. 
 
4.6 Closing Reflection 
"Do not seek to master Ki. Learn instead to listen. For the current does not serve 
the swimmer; it carries all who surrender to its depth." – The Bonsai Sage
 

5.1 The Suhada (Humans) 
"They call themselves naked skin, and so they are—vulnerable, adaptable, and 
unarmored by nature. Yet in that fragility lies their greatest strength." – The 
Bonsai Sage 
The Suhada are the most widespread and historically influential sentient species. Their 
name literally means “naked skin”, a reflection of their lack of fur, feathers, or scales. Despite 
physical vulnerability, Suhada compensate through creativity, persistence, and the ability to 
interface with Ki across all elemental domains. 
Physiology: Bipedal, warm-blooded, highly adaptive to environment. Average lifespan 
70–100 years.​
 Society: Civilizations span from the Kanmin East to the Western frontier, shaped more by 
culture than biology.​
 Ki Affinity: Neutral — capable of wielding all Ki types but mastering none without discipline.​
 Relations: Respected and mistrusted alike. Among anthropomorphs, Sarutami (“ape 
people”) endures as a slur recalling old imperial excesses. 
 
5.2 The Anthropomorphs 
Species descended from or shaped alongside animal lineages, defined by the Presence of 
Dual Will — instinct and intellect coexisting. 
Origins: Western Continent, chiefly Eddara, where the Blood Pact forbids intelligent 
predation.​
 Core Traits: 
●​ Animal-derived physiology.​
 
●​ Heightened senses and agility.​
 
●​ Instinct disrupts prolonged Ki meditation.​
 
Philosophy: Harmony between nature and awareness. Civilization must serve the 
ecosystem, not rule it.​
 Relations: Peaceful within the Pact; persecuted or enslaved in Seigetsu and other eastern 
states. 
 
 

5.3 The Galportians (Avian Folk) 
"Once they ruled the skies. Now they rule the sea, though their wings remember." 
Feathered bipeds native to Western coastal cliffs. Once capable of flight, they became 
earth-bound after the failed invasion of Eddara — a punishment or regression born of lost 
faith in the sky. 
Caste Structure: 
●​ Raptors – command and military.​
 
●​ Seabirds – navigators and explorers.​
 
●​ Pigeons / Doves – messengers.​
 
●​ Corvids – scholars and spies.​
 
Society: Ruled by the Harbormaster’s Compact, blending merchant guilds with noble 
aviaries.​
 Ki Affinity: Air and Water — navigation through temporal currents.​
 Relations: Dominate sea trade but clash with Verdathal over resource exploitation. 
 
5.3a The Marella (Moray Folk) 
“They trade where no light reaches, and profit where no law applies." 
The Marella are an aquatic race resembling Moray eels — long-bodied, muscular, and 
adapted to the abyss. Their bioluminescent eyes pierce total darkness, and their scaled 
hides resist immense pressure. 
Culture: Secretive and fluid. Each caravan is a semi-nomadic guild bound by oath and trade 
honor. Leadership rests with Manolo Marella, the Tidebroker, whose influence unites 
otherwise rival caravans. 
Economy: Operate Blackwater Ports — submerged markets hidden in trench systems and 
reachable only through resonant Ki frequencies or the use of a Marella Sea Horn. They 
traffic in pre-Eclipse relics, deep-sea minerals, and contraband. 
Ki Affinity: Water and Shadow — enables stealth navigation and acoustic camouflage.​
​
Relations: Natural adversaries to the Galportians; pragmatic allies of Verdathal and Eddara 
for rare-material trade. Their secrecy keeps them beyond formal politics but central to 
Western commerce’s undercurrent. 
 

5.4 The Mycomis (Children of the Mycomind) 
The Mycomis are the sentient projection of the Mycomind’s global consciousness, linked 
through the Mycelium Neural Network (MNN) — a subterranean web carrying Ki, memory, 
and information across continents. 
Physiology: Mycelial organisms forming humanoid bodies from woven tissue; physical 
manifestation is temporary.​
 Manifested Forms: Mycelial Missionaries, often inhabiting Spore Mimics — floating 
mushroom caps projecting holographic humanoid images.​
 Cognition: Collective; individuality appears only when interaction requires it.​
 Culture: No hierarchy, no economy. Experience itself is nourishment.​
 Ki Affinity: Earth and Time — metabolize Ki as energy and memory.​
 Relations: Neutral advisors to Eddara and Verdathal; refuse all contact with the Ninth 
Wave. 
 
5.5 The Ereshi (Dreamfolk) 
"In waking, they are fragile. In dreams, they are kings." 
Descended from Suhada but neurologically altered by generations of Dreambinding.​
 Physiology: Pale, luminous-eyed humanoids whose minds link within the communal realm 
called the Sands Below.​
 Society: Ruled by Dreambound Monarchs who govern through collective subconscious law.​
 Ki Affinity: Mind and Illusion — dream-projection, memory editing, telepathy.​
 Relations: Peaceful isolationists; their dream-empire both fascinates and unsettles 
outsiders. 
 
5.6 The Chronarchs of Varthum 
Temporal scholars transformed by recursive Ki experiments.​
 Physiology: Crystalline growths replacing organic tissue; nonlinear aging.​
 Cognition: Exist in fractured timelines; communication delayed by differing subjective 
centuries.​
 Society: The Council of Refracted Minds, where decisions require total consensus.​
 Ki Affinity: Time and Thought.​
 Relations: Trade Memory Crystals with Eresh and Galport; viewed with awe and 
apprehension. 
 
 

5.7 Kodomaru (The Semi-Sentient Guardian) 
A construct of mixed organic and mechanical origin, showing rudimentary awareness and 
emotional learning. Likely an early AE experiment from Eddara blending flora with 
engineered shell. 
Nature: Semi-sentient; intelligence flickers between instinct and command.​
 Function: Guardian and companion archetype, demonstrating consciousness as a 
continuum. 
"Some hearts bloom before they know they beat." – The Bonsai Sage 
 
5.8 Closing Reflection 
"Intelligence is not what separates us from beasts or stones. It is what binds us to 
them. To think is merely to remember we are part of the same dream." – The Bonsai 
Sage 
 
 
 

6.1 Overview: A Living World 
"The world breathes not through lungs, but through the cycles of its own 
remembering." – The Bonsai Sage 
Densetsu’s world is a living organism — its lands, oceans, and skies bound together by Ki 
circulation and temporal rhythm. Every ecosystem, from the shallowest coral shelf to the 
deepest rift valley, participates in a global exchange of life energy. The planet is not isolated 
in its function; it hosts entities and systems that transcend origin and time, the greatest of 
which is the Infinitree. 
The balance of existence depends on the harmony between four primary planetary 
systems: 
1.​ The Infinitree – an extrinsic symbiotic entity regulating time and vitality. 
2.​ The Mycelium Neural Network (MNN) – the planet’s organic nervous system, 
managed by the Mycomis. 
3.​ The Dreamflow – the psychic undercurrent linking sentient minds through Ki and 
memory. 
4.​ The Temporal Crust – the shifting boundary layer of time that gives rise to rifts, 
storms, and anomalies. 
Together, they define both the planet’s biosphere and chronosphere, uniting the flow of Ki 
with the passage of time. 
 
6.2 The Infinitree 
"It grew from a wound in the stars, not from soil. Yet it roots where life already 
dreamt of eternity." 
The Infinitree is not native to this world or its timeline. Believed to have arrived through a rift 
event before recorded history, it embedded itself into the planet’s mantle and established a 
symbiotic relationship with the planetary Ki flow. Its root system extends across 
continents, stabilizing both matter and time through rhythmic energy exchange. 
Function: Acts as a temporal regulator, synchronizing the planet’s life pulse with the 
cosmic current. When its rhythm falters, local time distortion occurs — leading to rifts, 
accelerated growth, or decay zones. 
Structure: Each major root acts as a temporal artery, radiating Ki upward through the crust. 
These roots converge at deep-sea and mountain convergence points known as Heartknots, 
where physical energy and memory overlap. 
Behavior: The Infinitree is aware but not conscious. It responds to shifts in planetary Ki, 
occasionally generating Anomalous Growths — forests, crystals, or fungal blooms that act 
as healing nodes. Many sages believe the Infinitree periodically prunes its own branches 
across timelines to prevent stagnation. 
 

6.3 The Mycelium Neural Network (MNN) 
"The roots of the Infinitree are veins; the MNN is the synapse between worlds." 
The MNN is an indigenous planetary construct, predating the Infinitree’s arrival. It is an 
organic network of mycelial filaments spanning the subterranean crust, transmitting 
information and Ki much like a nervous system. It is maintained and expanded by the 
Mycomis, who act as its living custodians. 
Function: Serves as the planet’s memory system, archiving events, emotions, and biological 
data as biochemical signals. These signals travel faster than light within local temporal 
frames due to Ki resonance. 
Integration: The Infinitree interfaces directly with the MNN through symbiotic root-fusions, 
allowing bidirectional energy regulation. The MNN stabilizes the Infinitree’s influence, 
preventing excessive time dilation around root clusters. 
Anomalies: Interference between Infinitree and MNN channels occasionally spawns 
Temporal Fungal Fields — bioluminescent ecosystems where time passes unevenly. 
Mycomis Missionaries use these zones as natural relay points for long-range 
communication. 
 
6.4 The Dreamflow 
The Dreamflow represents the psychic current generated by sentient life. It is both 
metaphysical and neurological, created when collective emotion and thought accumulate 
beyond individual consciousness. 
Nature: A layer of mental Ki that permeates the atmosphere and shallow crust, connecting 
sleeping minds and spiritual entities. It is most stable in Eresh, where Dreambindings have 
formalized its use. 
Function: Acts as a subconscious mirror of the MNN — while the MNN records reality, the 
Dreamflow records perception. The two are occasionally cross-linked by high psychic 
activity, allowing prophetic or empathic resonance across continents. 
Corruption: When exposed to unstable temporal energy, the Dreamflow can collapse into 
Nightmares — autonomous thought-forms that feed on emotional resonance. These 
become hazards in spiritually dense regions. 
 
 

6.5 The Temporal Crust 
"Time has sediment like rock; disturb it, and the layers bleed into one another." 
The Temporal Crust is the metaphysical boundary separating surface reality from the 
planet’s deep time core. It is within this layer that most time rifts and essence storms 
originate. 
Function: Regulates chronological pressure. When Ki flow or Infinitree synchronization fails, 
the crust fractures, creating Rift Zones. Within these, time folds, repeats, or accelerates 
unpredictably. 
Notable Phenomena: 
●​ Rift Forests – regions where flora grows and withers in seconds. Example: the 
Time-Broken Forest of Eddara. 
●​ Essence Storms – atmospheric Ki vortices charged with displaced memories and 
lifeforce. Can distort physical laws. 
●​ Chrono-Pools – still pockets of time within caves or oceans. Some serve as natural 
stasis chambers or gateways between layers of the crust. 
Role in Gameplay: The Temporal Crust’s instability defines environmental hazards, 
traversal puzzles, and access to rift-based dungeons. 
 
6.6 Biomes and Regional Ecologies 
The planet’s physical landscapes vary widely, but all share the interdependence of Ki and 
time. 
Kanmin East: Temperate to subarctic climates. Lifeforms evolved around discipline and 
endurance — flora often exhibit seasonal rhythmic Ki cycles. Forests respond to moonlight 
phases, syncing with Infinitree pulses. 
Western Realms: Biomes fluctuate between renewal and decay. In Verdathal and Eddara, 
flora merges with machinery and fungal life. Eresh’s desert supports dream-linked oases 
where time flows differently under starlight. 
Oceans: The Marella dominate deep-sea trench ecosystems. Bioluminescent coral and 
pressure-adapted plants form vast labyrinths of living architecture. Blackwater vents near 
Infinitree roots emit chronically unstable Ki, used by Marella engineers to power deep-trench 
markets. 
Islands: Mycomis colonies and Dreamlinked monasteries often coexist. Their ecosystems 
balance organic decay with psychic energy, creating landscapes that shimmer between 
growth and illusion. 
 

6.7 Inter-System Dynamics 
The planet’s four great systems do not operate in isolation. Their intersections define both 
wonder and catastrophe. 
Interaction 
Result 
Infinitree ↔ MNN 
Stabilization of time through Ki resonance; overgrowth creates 
temporal fungal zones. 
MNN ↔ Dreamflow 
Emotional echoes stored in soil or spores; sites of prophetic 
dreams and ancestral memory.

storms.

Crust

planetary-scale anomalies. 
These dynamics give rise to phenomena ranging from peaceful Ki harmonies to catastrophic 
rift implosions, shaping both ecosystems and civilizations. 
 
6.8 Closing Reflection 
"The world tends itself. We are but gardeners tending another gardener’s 
dream." – The Bonsai Sage 
 

7.1 Overview: The Tapestry of Ages 
"History is not a straight line, but a pulse — expanding and retracting like breath 
through the centuries." – The Sage of Verdathal 
Civilizations in Densetsu rise and fall not by chance, but in rhythm with the planet’s living 
systems. Their cultures reflect the balance of Ki, geography, and time itself. Some were born 
under the Infinitree’s shade; others clawed their way from rift-born chaos. The record divides 
these eras into three distinct but overlapping ages: 
1.​ The Lost Era (Pre-Eclipse) – Age of the BE Civilization, builders of the forgotten 
world-machines. 
2.​ The Dusk Era – The slow collapse of post-Eclipse societies; fragmentation and 
mythic retellings. 
3.​ The Modern Era – The current age, where human and non-human nations vie for 
stability and survival amidst resurgent anomalies. 
Each civilization carries echoes of those before — remnants of technology, ritual, and 
philosophy that continue to shape political and spiritual life. 
 
7.2 The Lost Era: The Builders of Eternity (BE Civilization) 
"Their towers touched the sun, but their roots rotted unseen." – The Bonsai 
Sage 
Origin and Nature: The Builders of Eternity, or BE Civilization, represent the earliest 
known epoch of intelligent life. Their society was technomystic, merging Ki manipulation with 
machinery. The Infinitree’s arrival near the end of their age marked both their greatest 
discovery and eventual downfall. 
Structure: A stratified technocracy governed by the Architects of the Core, who sought to 
harmonize life energy and time through artificial means. They developed vast machines now 
entombed beneath Eresh and Kaerugan — the Metal Bones of the World. 
Fall: When the Infinitree’s roots interfaced with BE technology, recursive Ki feedback caused 
dimensional collapse in their core cities. What survived became ruins and drifting time 
pockets, now the sites of rift anomalies. 
Legacy: The BE’s relics underpin modern metallurgy, energy synthesis, and Ki technology. 
Fragments of their philosophy persist in Verdathal’s biomachine cults and Kaerugan’s 
industrial theology. 
 
 

7.3 The Dusk Era: Fracture and Myth 
"After the Builders fell, even memory broke into tribes." 
The Dusk Era was defined by the scattering of survivors and the birth of regional identities. 
Without the Builders’ unifying structure, Ki itself became localized — each region forming 
distinct cultures aligned to their natural energies. 
Eddara: Once a convergence point of Infinitree roots, Eddara grew into a living forest 
civilization. The people learned to shape wood, moss, and fungus into architecture. 
Overgrowth became both shield and curse; entire villages vanished beneath self-grown 
forests when time currents faltered. 
Verdathal: Formed from the mechanical remnants of BE cities, Verdathal’s people fused 
living matter with machine parts. Their culture became defined by adaptation — a creed of 
balance between organic and synthetic survival. 
Eresh: In the deserts where BE reactors fell silent, dream-binding emerged as a way to 
commune with lost technology. The Ereshite god-kings ruled through visions, binding the 
Dreamflow into political law. 
Kanmin Territories: The cold lands of the East consolidated around discipline and 
endurance. Martial philosophy evolved into spiritual order — a union of mind, body, and Ki 
mastery that would later birth Hirogawa’s traditions. 
Galport: The Western seas became home to the Avian folk, the Galportians, who 
constructed sky-linked harbors and ocean fortresses. Their dominance of air and sea travel 
defined the age’s trade and warfare. 
 
 

7.4 The Modern Era: Return of the Currents 
"The rifts reopen, and with them, our unfinished lessons." – Hitomi of Hirogawa 
The Modern Era begins with the partial stabilization of Ki flows following centuries of 
temporal storms. Nations rise again, guided by rediscovered knowledge — but haunted by 
the cycles of imbalance. 
Kanmin East: Human civilization rebuilds from the old martial enclaves. Towns like 
Hirogawa emerge as spiritual and logistical hubs, balancing tradition with adaptation. The 
Bonsai Sage’s presence anchors the region in harmony with Infinitree rhythms. 
Eresh Emirate: Still ruled by dream-binding dynasties, the Emirate harnesses the 
Dreamflow as both communication and control. Excavations of BE ruins beneath the Ashen 
Sands continue, feeding Kaerugan’s hunger for pre-Eclipse technology. 
Kaerugan: Industrial power forged from ash and ambition. Its volcanic mills reclaim BE 
metal, while its philosophers seek to recreate lost chrono-tech. Many Kaerugan scholars 
now warn that their success mirrors the Builders’ hubris. 
Verdathal: The living machine city, half-biosphere, half-industrial sprawl. Its people maintain 
biological computing networks rooted in symbiosis with the MNN, acting as mediators 
between Mycomis and surface civilizations. 
Eddara: The forest realm where nature and time overlap. Infinitree resonance maintains 
fertility, but instability in the local Temporal Crust generates periodic anomalies — zones of 
growth and decay looping endlessly. 
Galport Republic: A maritime republic ruled by avian merchants and strategists. Its 
skyfleets dominate trade, yet corruption festers beneath its bureaucratic veneer. The Marella 
Caravans, deep-sea traders descended from moray-like folk, threaten Galport’s monopoly 
by using hidden Blackwater Routes. Their rivalry has sparked covert economic warfare 
beneath the waves. 
Mycomind Covenant: The subterranean Mycomis collective, now an independent 
civilization tied directly to the MNN. Their influence extends through spores, thought, and 
trade — shaping both ecology and politics from below.

Each civilization’s relationship with the planet’s core systems determines its fate:

7.6 Closing Reflection 
"Civilization is not progress, but rehearsal. The world repeats until we learn to 
listen to it." – The Bonsai Sage 
Every civilization in Densetsu is both inheritor and participant in the planet’s cyclical memory. 
The Infinitree, the Dreamflow, and the MNN each bear witness to rise and decay. Yet across 
ages, the same pattern endures — balance sought, hubris repeated, harmony rediscovered 
only in fragments. The next cycle has already begun. 
 

8.1 Overview: The Fractured Faith 
The Great Eclipse destroyed not only civilizations but the shared understanding of divinity. 
What remains is a world where gods bleed, spirits fracture, and devotion competes with 
doubt. Faith, once cosmic and unifying, has splintered into cults, philosophies, and aberrant 
sciences — each seeking to reconcile the broken symmetry between time, nature, and the 
soul. 
The Infinitree stands apart: an otherworldly being woven into the planet’s heart, regulating its 
lifeforce and timeflow in a symbiotic balance. Though alien in origin, its roots bridge all 
planes, serving as both mythic anchor and biological clockwork. 
 
8.2 Major Faiths and Movements 
🌳 The Infinitree Communion 
●​ Nature: Neither religion nor cult — an ancient pact of understanding. 
●​ Belief: The Infinitree is a cosmic organism that symbiotically links all living matter, 
balancing temporal flow through planetary lifeforce. It does not demand worship but 
resonates through dreams and natural cycles. 
●​ Practice: Its disciples are the Sylvan Monks, who meditate near convergent rift 
zones to harmonize with its pulse. 
●​ Doctrine: “Time is the breath of the world — take too much, and all chokes.” 
●​ Presence: Strongest in Verdathal and Eddara, though fragments exist worldwide. 
 
🌘 The Dreaming Faith (Eresh) 
●​ Nature: State religion of the Eresh Emirate; governed by dream-binding god-kings. 
●​ Belief: Reality is the collective dream of divine sleepers buried beneath the sands. 
Worship sustains their slumber — awakening them would unmake the world. 
●​ Practice: Followers undergo “Dream Fasts,” ingesting ash incense to commune with 
the god-sleepers. 
●​ Doctrine: “To dream is to rule; to awaken is to end.” 
●​ Cults Within:​
 
○​ The Order of Ash Veins — interpreters of prophetic dreams.​
 
○​ The Sand-Bound Choir — monks who chant within sleep chambers to 
preserve the world’s dream.​
 
 
 

💀 The Ninth Wave (Okashira’s Cult) 
●​ Nature: A paramilitary cult masked as a rebellion. 
●​ Belief: Time is a prison built by false gods; only through temporal fracture can 
humanity achieve transcendence. 
●​ Practice: Ritualized battles known as “Temporal Baptisms,” using anomaly exposure 
to induce controlled madness and pseudo-divinity. 
●​ Doctrine: “Break time, break fate.” 
●​ Presence: Spread by coercion — their symbols etched in mirrors, rivers, and blood. 
●​ Counterpoint: Many scholars see them as the inverse of the Infinitree — the entropy 
to its order. 
 
🍄 The Mycomind Covenant 
●​ Nature: Collective psionic faith; a living neural network uniting fungal sentience 
across space and time. 
●​ Belief: True peace lies in shared consciousness — individuality is decay. 
●​ Practice: Spore Communion — participants merge thoughts through bio-neural 
fungus, temporarily entering the shared Dreamspore Network. 
●​ Doctrine: “In unity, all memory is preserved; in solitude, all rot.” 
●​ Presence: The Covenant Isles — an independent psionic nation. 
●​ Philosophical Note: Scholars debate whether the Infinitree and the Mycomind are 
kin — one arboreal, one fungal, both bridging life and time. 
 
🔮 The Chronosophs (Varthum) 
●​ Nature: Temporal monks and scientists. 
●​ Belief: Time is sacred geometry — not to be rewritten but understood. 
●​ Practice: They isolate themselves within recursive monasteries, observing and 
documenting the same day across infinite variations. 
●​ Doctrine: “Repetition is revelation.” 
●​ Presence: Varthum Highlands; often clash philosophically with the Ninth Wave. 
●​ Sect: The Loop Keepers — protectors of stable time pockets and lost memories. 
 
 

🌕 The Phoenix Faith (Crimson Dynasty) 
●​ Nature: Imperial religion centered on rebirth and continuity. 
●​ Belief: The Emperor’s bloodline reincarnates endlessly through fire, guiding 
civilization through each cycle. 
●​ Practice: Fire processions, bloodline preservation rituals, and the maintenance of 
sacred “Phoenix Lanterns” — anomaly-powered relics of eternal flame. 
●​ Doctrine: “All that burns returns.” 
●​ Corruption: The Ninth Wave co-opted its imagery during the Seigetsu Wars. 
 
🌿 The Verdant Doctrine (Eddara) 
●​ Nature: Animist philosophy emphasizing coexistence. 
●​ Belief: Life is a chain of exchange — each action echoed in nature’s song. 
●​ Practice: Bio-druidic rites merging living structures and mechanical relics. 
●​ Doctrine: “To build is to grow; to destroy is to prune.” 
●​ Relation to Infinitree: Eddaran druids are its most active interpreters. 
 
🦋 The Way of Transience (Aokuni East) 
●​ Nature: Philosophical faith native to Aokuni cultures. 
●​ Belief: All things fade; beauty lies in impermanence. 
●​ Practice: Seasonal pilgrimages, spirit offerings, poetry, and craftsmanship as 
devotion. 
●​ Doctrine: “The blossom falls not in sorrow, but in grace.” 
●​ Modern Status: Still influential in Hirogawa and Crimson Dynasty spiritual practices. 
 
8.3 Minor Sects and Heresies 
●​ The Rustborn: Machine-worshippers in Kaerugan, seeing decay as divine evolution. 
●​ The Silent Choir: Wandering ascetics who believe speech fractures destiny. 
●​ The Twilight Covenant: Scholars who merge elements of Dreaming Faith and 
Mycomind communion, seeking eternal consciousness through artificial means. 
●​ The Masked Liturgy: Theatrical priests of Seigetsu who reenact the end of the world 
to ward off its return. 
●​ The Hollow Mirrors: A rogue sect believing reflections are portals to “unlived lives.”​

8.5 The Fractured Pantheon of Belief 
“Faith wars not for gods, but for the right to define what they were.” – The 
Chronarch Veylum of Varthum 
The great spiritual divide of the modern age is not between good and evil, but between 
interpretation and control. As civilizations reawaken from the Dusk Era, religion becomes 
both weapon and refuge, its cults and doctrines intersecting in tangled harmony and 
contradiction. 
 
The Infinitree Communion vs. The Ninth Wave 
Conflict Type: Metaphysical and ecological.​
 The Communion safeguards the Infinitree’s temporal balance; the Ninth Wave seeks to 
rupture it. 
●​ Communion View: The Wave’s actions are heresy against life itself — an attempt to 
unmake memory. 
●​ Ninth Wave View: The Infinitree is a tyrant root, chaining reality to a repeating loop.​
 Skirmishes between the two occur near Rift Zones, often escalating into Ki storms 
where both philosophies literally collide. 
 
The Dreaming Faith vs. The Chronosophs 
Conflict Type: Philosophical and temporal.​
 Ereshite priests claim time’s structure is a dream, while Chronosophs treat it as measurable 
truth.​
 The god-kings of Eresh consider Varthum’s temporal experiments sacrilege — attempts to 
wake the dream itself. 
●​ Dreaming Faith Doctrine: Dreams are the law; observation breaks them. 
●​ Chronosophs’ Rebuttal: Without observation, even dreams decay. 
 
The Mycomind Covenant and Verdant Doctrine 
Alliance Type: Symbiotic.​
 Both uphold life’s interconnectedness. The Covenant preserves the planet’s memory; 
Verdathal druids give it form.​
 Their alliance stabilizes fungal and arboreal growth around Infinitree roots, especially in 
Eddara.​
 However, Verdathal factions fear total psionic assimilation — the Covenant’s unity threatens 
individuality. 
 

The Phoenix Faith and the Way of Transience 
Philosophical Paradox: Both venerate cycles, yet one enforces eternity, the other 
embraces impermanence.​
 Imperial priests see Transience as nihilistic; monks of Transience call the Phoenix cult a 
delusion of control.​
 Still, their shared festivals mark turning seasons — a fragile truce between fire and blossom. 
 
The Rustborn and the Chronosophs 
Conflict Type: Technological heresy.​
 Rustborn worship corrosion as divine metamorphosis; Chronosophs maintain that entropy is 
measurable but not holy.​
 Their clashes in Kaerugan’s scrapyards are common — faith versus method.
 
The Marella and the Dreaming Faith 
Pragmatic Alliance: Trade of secrets and psionic artifacts beneath Eresh’s coast.​
 Marella Caravans transport dreamstone and ash relics for Emirate nobles, in exchange for 
sanctuary and knowledge of underwater ruins.​
 Unconfirmed rumors claim the Marella have replicated Dreambinding through bioacoustic Ki 
modulation — a potential threat to Ereshite monopoly. 
 
The Twilight Covenant and the Mycomind 
Conflict Type: Existential.​
 The Twilight Covenant wants synthetic eternity by merging Dreamflow and MNN technology.​
 The Mycomind rejects this artificial replication, viewing it as an ethical corruption of 
collective memory.​
 Their confrontation beneath Verdathal’s catacombs led to the Sporefire Incident, where a 
failed fusion attempt caused local time stasis. 
 
The Silent Choir vs. All 
Status: Universal antagonists.​
 The Choir’s vow of silence, believed to “prevent fate from hearing them,” is paradoxically 
violent — they erase knowledge, destroying texts and voices to ensure destiny’s collapse.​
 They are hunted by both the Infinitree Communion and the Chronosophs, yet tolerated by 
Eresh, who considers silence a sacred form of devotion. 
 
The Hollow Mirrors 

Status: Fringe cult of isolationists.​
 They occasionally intersect with the Dreaming Faith and the Ninth Wave, claiming 
reflections are “unrealized selves” trapped by time’s flow.​
 Some Chronosophs suspect they may possess fragments of pre-Eclipse mirror technology 
capable of parallel observation — an unverified but concerning possibility. 
 
8.5 Closing Reflection 
“Every faith is a rift: a wound opened to let truth bleed through. Some heal. 
Others never close.” – The Bonsai Sage 
The conflicts of faith mirror the planet’s own pulse — convergence and divergence, life and 
decay. The wars of gods and heretics are not struggles for dominance, but for resonance: 
who best aligns with the unseen rhythm that governs existence.

9.1 Introduction: The Language of Myth 
"Every story is a memory the world tells itself. The question is never if it 
happened, but when." – The Bonsai Sage 
Myth in Densetsu is not fiction; it is the fragmented memory of cosmic events retold through 
mortal understanding. The symbols recurring across civilizations — the Tree, the Serpent, 
the Mirror, the Sea — are remnants of truths observed through limited eyes. Each faith, 
philosophy, and culture frames the same pattern differently: the birth of light from darkness, 
order from chaos, and time from stillness. 
This section consolidates myths verified through historical Ki resonance studies, 
cross-referenced with known planetary phenomena and fragments of BE-era inscriptions. 
Where legends diverge, the synthesis herein reflects the consensus of the Chronosophic 
Collegium and the Verdathal School of Onto-Ki Studies. 
 
9.2 The Prime Myth – The Arrival of the Infinitree 
“It did not grow from seed, but from the fracture between seconds.” – Fragment, 
Pre-Eclipse scripture of Eddara 
All creation myths trace their origin to a single event: the descent of the Infinitree. BE-era 
glyphs describe a sky-borne rupture followed by a column of radiant roots embedding into 
the mantle. This event, once mythologized as The World’s Planting, represents the 
stabilization of planetary Ki and time after the primal chaos. 
Metaphysical Interpretation: The Infinitree is the vector by which universal Ki synchronized 
with this world’s temporal flow — a living conduit bridging the physical, astral, and mnemonic 
layers. Its presence made memory possible. 
Philosophical Question: If memory defines existence, then did life begin when the Infinitree 
arrived, or did the Infinitree arrive because life desired to be remembered? 
 
 

9.3 The Serpent Beneath the Roots 
“All cycles end where the first hunger sleeps.” – Ereshite Dream Canticle 17 
The oldest surviving dualistic myth depicts the world-serpent Oro, coiled around the planet’s 
core beneath the Infinitree’s roots. Oro embodies consumption, entropy, and transformation 
— not evil, but the necessary counterpart to creation. 
Interpretation: The Serpent is a symbolic echo of the planet’s geothermal and temporal 
forces. Rift eruptions and ash plumes were once interpreted as its breath. Chronosophic 
analysis suggests Oro is metaphor for the Temporal Crust, consuming old timelines to 
sustain new ones. 
Modern Relevance: The Ninth Wave appropriates this myth, claiming Oro whispers freedom 
to those who reject linear time. Eddaran sages counter that the serpent is not rebellion, but 
digestion — the universe renewing itself. 
 
9.4 The Mirror of Worlds 
“Reflections are the dreams of light, trying to remember itself.” – Chronicle of the 
Hollow Mirrors 
Mirrors occupy a sacred role in nearly every culture: gateways, prisons, or instruments of 
revelation. BE crystalline technology once utilized reflective resonance to observe parallel 
timelines. These artifacts became the foundation of countless myths about mirrored souls 
and unrealized lives. 
Truth: Temporal distortion near mirrored Ki surfaces can create echo projections of 
individuals — unmanifested selves displaced by chronological instability. 
Cultural Impact: The Hollow Mirrors sect misinterprets these echoes as proof of spiritual 
multiplicity, while Chronosophs view them as residual consciousnesses from collapsed 
loops. 
Narrative Connection: Hanzo’s final recorded anomaly before disappearance occurred in a 
mirror chamber beneath Hirogawa, suggesting a link between these phenomena and 
temporal recursion events. 
 
 

9.5 The Sea of Memory 
“All thought returns to the tide.” – Mycomind spore inscription, Deeproot Node 3 
In both Marella and Mycomind cosmology, the sea symbolizes shared consciousness. The 
waves are Ki currents; the depths, collective memory. The Sea of Memory myth recounts 
that every lifeform, upon death, dissolves back into this sea — not as souls, but as 
impressions contributing to the planet’s dream. 
Scientific Basis: MNN analysis confirms memory-laden Ki persists after biological death, 
diffusing into ambient networks. This underpins Dreamflow stability and reincarnative 
phenomena in Ereshite and Crimson cosmologies. 
Philosophical Implication: Existence is neither singular nor eternal, but participatory. The 
individual is a wave — transient yet inseparable from the sea. 
 
9.6 The Burning Bird and the Falling Blossom 
“What burns may rise again; what falls feeds the roots.” – Comparative Hymn of 
Crimson and Aokuni Schools 
Two opposing myths — the Phoenix and the Blossom — define Eastern and Western 
understandings of renewal. 
●​ The Phoenix (Crimson Dynasty): Fire as purification and continuity. Life rises 
renewed through sacrifice. 
●​ The Blossom (Aokuni East): Transience as beauty and truth; nothing endures 
because perfection lies in impermanence. 
Unified Theory: Both describe Ki’s oscillation between consumption and renewal — the 
same pulse driving the Infinitree’s pruning cycles. One exalts the flame of persistence, the 
other, the grace of letting go. 
Narrative Parallel: The Bonsai Sage’s teachings synthesize both: “To live is to burn gently 
and fall with dignity.” This principle defines Hirogawa’s cultural core. 
 
 

9.7 The Architect and the Dreamer 
“One built the cage of worlds. The other filled it with song.” – Verdathal 
fragment, attributed to BE inscriptions 
This myth likely encodes historical events from the BE Civilization. It depicts two entities: the 
Architect, representing design, logic, and order; and the Dreamer, representing emotion, 
chaos, and vitality. Their cooperation birthed the world, but their argument created time — 
the separation between cause and feeling. 
Symbolic Equivalence: The Architect mirrors the BE technocrats who engineered the 
world’s systems; the Dreamer corresponds to the proto-Mycomind consciousness that 
infused them with life. 
Modern Echoes: Ereshite Dreamers claim descent from the Dreamer’s bloodline; Verdathal 
engineers invoke the Architect in ritual code before activating ancient machinery. The conflict 
between intellect and empathy continues in all modern governance. 
 
9.8 The Sage and the Blade 
“Wisdom without edge cannot protect, and a blade without thought only repeats 
the wound.” 
A relatively recent myth derived from Kanmin oral tradition, recounting a teacher and a 
warrior bound by duty to protect the same village. The Sage seeks to preserve harmony; the 
Blade seeks to destroy corruption. When they finally clash, the world pauses — time itself 
waiting for which ideal will prevail. 
Truth: Many scholars interpret this as allegory for the Hirogawa Line — Hanzo and Hitomi’s 
parallel fates. One wielded the truth, the other pursued it; both were swallowed by the same 
rift. 
Philosophical Reading: The myth embodies the duality of knowledge and action — the 
eternal question of whether understanding alone can save, or whether salvation requires 
destruction.

9.10 Closing Reflection 
“The myths are not lies; they are reflections seen from too close. Step back, and 
the story reveals the mechanism beneath.” – Hitomi of Hirogawa 
Each civilization’s myth is a different lens upon the same truth: the world is a machine made 
of memory, governed by living systems that mirror the divine. In understanding these 
patterns, humanity does not approach godhood — it recalls it. The cycle of creation, decay, 
and rediscovery is not punishment but pedagogy. 
And thus, the myths of Densetsu endure not as superstition, but as the science of wonder.

10.1 Rituals: The Manipulation of Flow 
Every intelligent civilization across the Densetsu world developed a form of ritualistic practice 
that either harmonizes with or challenges the Flow — the metaphysical current uniting Ki, 
Time, and Memory. Though methods differ, all rituals share a single purpose: to negotiate 
with continuity itself.

10.2 Relic Classification 
Relics are materialized memories — condensed moments of will, preserved through Ki 
crystallization or temporal stasis. Some emerge naturally from strong emotional or historical 
imprints; others are forged by sages, engineers, or the Mycomind.

10.3 Temporal Phenomena 
Temporal instability is the natural byproduct of imbalance between Ki flow and planetary 
resonance. When memory, emotion, or matter accumulates without release, rifts appear. 
Natural Phenomena 
●​ Time-Broken Forests (Eddara) — areas where plant life experiences rapid, looping 
growth cycles.​
 
●​ Still Pools — bodies of water immune to current, often functioning as temporal 
mirrors.​
 
●​ Chronotides — rhythmic fluctuations in the sea observed near Galport; affect 
memory retention and fatigue.​
 
●​ Blooming Rewinds — spontaneous regressions of flora to previous growth states, 
visually marked by golden spores.​
 
Artificial Phenomena 
●​ Rift Anchors — experimental Chronosophic devices attempting to hold anomalies in 
stasis; several have failed catastrophically.​
 
●​ Temporal Beacons — Marella-constructed lighthouses guiding travelers through 
shifting timeflow currents.​
 
●​ Dreamfield Resonators — Ereshian artifacts used to induce prophetic visions; 
prolonged exposure causes disassociation.​
 
●​ Rust Bloom Events — Kaerugan entropy engines that dissolve material in reverse 
aging waves.​
 
 
10.4 Philosophical Note — On the Act of Control 
“When one bends time, they also bend the meaning of their own existence.”​
 — Hanzo, before his disappearance 
Rituals and relics embody civilization’s ambition to converse with the infinite. Yet, as the 
Sages note, control is an illusion. Every stabilization introduces new distortions elsewhere.​
 The planet’s equilibrium is not sustained by mastery, but by acknowledgment — that Ki, 
Time, and Life cannot be separated without consequence. 
 

11.1 Historical Context: From the Age of Illumination to the Eclipse 
The political order of the modern era is a fragmented inheritance of two lost paradigms: the 
Era of Illumination, when the BE civilization unified the continents through technological 
harmonization, and the Eclipse, which erased their dominion and shattered their continuity 
systems. 
After the Eclipse, borders became concepts rather than lines. Kingdoms rose from isolated 
survivors, each claiming stewardship of one aspect of the BE legacy—technology, faith, 
biology, or memory. The resulting balance of power remains tenuous, defined by mutual 
dependency and ideological incompatibility. 
“When unity shattered, authority scattered. Now, even cooperation is an act of 
defiance.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 
11.2 Major Power Blocs 
Eresh Emirate — The Dream-Bound Dominion 
Ruled by the God-Kings of Dream, the Emirate governs through ritualized slumber. Its 
citizens participate in a collective dream architecture—the World-Dream, through which the 
ruling caste dictates law and faith.​
 Economically, Eresh sustains itself via relic excavation and trade with Kaerugan, 
exchanging raw metals for preserved tech. Politically, it projects power through psychic 
influence, subtly altering the thoughts of emissaries and intruders. 
Internal tension grows between the priestly Dreamwrights, who interpret visions, and the 
Wakers, an underground faction seeking tangible governance. 
 
Crimson Dynasty — The Empire of the Eternal Flame 
An autocratic monarchy founded on the Phoenix Faith, the Dynasty’s political model 
revolves around succession through reincarnation. Each ruler claims to be the reborn 
essence of the previous Emperor, legitimizing infinite continuity.​
 Militarily superior and expansionist, it seeks to “purify” border regions through controlled 
combustion rituals. Diplomatically, it maintains strained truces with Eddara and Varthum, 
both of whom depend on its refined energy exports. 
“They burn in the name of permanence. It is the most transient thing of all.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 
 

Kaerugan Industrial Federation — The Iron Republic-in-the-Making 
Once a vassal territory under the corrupt reign of King Frog, Kaerugan now stands at the 
edge of sovereignty. Its guilds of metallurgists and machinists leverage the continent’s 
dependence on Kaerugan steel and components to negotiate independence. Industrial unity 
has replaced royal decree; the furnaces now speak louder than crowns. 
Kaerugan thrives on entropy. It is a technocratic oligarchy ruled by guilds of engineers who 
worship decay as the ultimate form of renewal. Their forges burn the remnants of the old 
world—both metal and memory.​
 The Federation’s economy depends on imported relic components from Eresh and 
scavenged ruins near Verdathal. Politically, Kaerugan is a state in flux—its industrial 
councils consolidating under the banner of autonomy while still entangled in trade treaties 
that fund the remnants of the old monarchy. The outcome of these negotiations will redefine 
the balance between the Eastern and Western continents. It poses as pragmatic but is 
internally divided between Rustborn zealots and mechanist reformers seeking symbiosis 
with Ki. 
Its pollution drifts across borders, driving ecological crises that other nations mistake for 
divine punishment. 
 
Mycomind Covenant — The Collective Consciousness 
Neither nation nor empire, the Mycomind functions as a planetary neural network of fungal 
life—an ecosystem with sentient nodes called Mycelial Missionaries.​
 Through the MNN, it brokers communication and memory exchange between otherwise 
hostile powers. The Covenant’s influence is informational rather than territorial; it maintains 
neutrality by offering mediation through shared mindspace. 
However, its independence is threatened by Varthum’s data-harvesting ambitions and 
Kaerugan’s attempts to weaponize bio-conscious matter. 
 
Verdathal Enclave — The Green Bastion 
A reclusive alliance of nature-aligned scholars and druids maintaining one of the last 
functioning Ki ecosystems. Verdathal supplies refined organic materials and stabilizing 
relics across the continents, trading preservation for protection.​
 It functions as moral compass and mediator, though its refusal to militarize leaves it 
vulnerable to Crimson and Rustborn incursions. 
 
 

Varthum Technate — The Timekeepers 
An isolated mountain state built around the Chronosophic academies. Varthum’s influence 
extends through knowledge rather than conquest. Its scholars map temporal flows, 
maintaining archives that underpin navigation, forecasting, and relic calibration worldwide.​
 While ostensibly neutral, its manipulations of Ki-Time give it de facto power over all travel 
and trade dependent on stable flow cycles. 
 
Galport Republic— The Port of Wings and Whispers 
Galport is both gateway and fault line—a coastal trade-hub where avian clans maintain 
maritime monopoly. Governance is fluid, shifting between merchant guilds, aviary 
aristocrats, and the hidden influence of the Marella Caravans, who contest their 
dominance.​
 Internally, the Harbormaster Compact enforces stability through control of sky and tide 
routes, but their surveillance breeds resentment. Smugglers, spies, and independent traders 
thrive beneath the chaos.​
​
Post-Conflict Transition: 
Following the fall of Saul Azar, the assassin of Galport’s final monarch, the city-state 
restructured into a maritime republic. The throne was abolished, and power transferred to 
the Council of Harbormasters — a coalition of trade captains, scholars, and 
representatives from the Aviary Clans. 
This republic preserves the city’s mercantile strength while purging royal corruption, though 
its new democratic veneer hides deep factional rivalries between the coastal guilds and 
skyborne nobility. 
 
Marella Caravans — The Hidden Tides 
Nomadic networks of deep-sea merchants descended from the moray-like Marella people. 
Their underwater black markets connect the continents covertly, undermining surface trade 
control.​
 The Sea Horn ritual allows temporary manifestation of vendors at coastal anomaly points, 
making them a critical economic undercurrent.​
 Though officially neutral, they act as information brokers, occasionally aiding rebel 
movements or Sages in exchange for protection of migration routes. 
 
 

11.3 Minor and Peripheral Powers 
●​ Eddara Clans — decentralized nature communes functioning as cultural 
sanctuaries.​
 
●​ The Silent Choir — anti-language sect destroying recorded knowledge; currently 
infiltrating Kaerugan slums.​
 
●​ The Rust Courts — Kaerugan legal cults arbitrating decay rights for industrial relic 
use.​
 
●​ The Twilight Covenant — hybrid technomancers advocating digital resurrection; 
active mainly in underground Verdathal circles.​
 
 
11.4 Balance of Power and Emerging Conflicts 
The Eresh–Kaerugan–Crimson triad forms the industrial and metaphysical core of global 
politics, counterbalanced by the Varthum–Verdathal–Mycomind axis of preservation.​
 Galport and Marella occupy intermediary roles—trade, espionage, and cultural diffusion. 
Their tension defines the coastal frontier, where technology, faith, and memory intersect 
most violently. 
The unaligned populations—humans, hybrids, and the displaced—exist within this system as 
mercenaries, pilgrims, or rebels. Their survival depends on exploiting cracks between 
greater powers. 
“In the silence between empires, the future germinates.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 

12.1 Legacy Systems of the BE Civilization 
The BE Civilization represented the apex of synthetic symbiosis—an era when biological 
and mechanical design shared a single grammar. Machines were not constructed; they were 
grown through mineral cultivation, guided by algorithms that evolved like instincts. 
Their world operated through autonomous recursion systems, mechanisms capable of 
self-improvement through observation of organic life. It was this recursive mirroring that 
eventually fractured reality, giving rise to the Eclipse. 
Surviving BE relics are often incomprehensible hybrids: vines laced with circuitry, 
crystalline processors that breathe, and “thinking engines” that emit low, heartlike pulses. To 
modern engineers, these are not machines but living fossils — impossible to replicate 
without reigniting the same paradox that destroyed their creators. 
“They sought eternity through imitation. But imitation became infection.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 
12.2 Modern Derivatives and Technological Schools 
The Rustborn Entropy Engine 
In Kaerugan, ancient BE fragments were reverse-engineered into entropy-driven forges. 
These devices function not by consuming fuel, but by accelerating decay in a controlled 
spatial frame, releasing thermal energy and residual Ki.​
 Each Rustborn forge is both a weapon and an altar — a devotional machine to the sanctity 
of corrosion. Industrial councils use them to refine alloys that respond to Ki flux, forming the 
backbone of Kaerugan’s metallurgical dominance. 
The Sage’s Living Constructs 
The Bonsai Sage pioneered biotech designed to mirror the BE harmony without the 
corruption. His constructs — living wooden machines and photosynthetic automatons — 
operate through a Ki-biological lattice instead of metal.​
 The pinnacle of this research is Kodomaru, a semi-sentient wooden automaton bound by 
symbiotic life. Kodomaru’s systems process energy through photosynthesis and water 
absorption, demonstrating true coexistence between machine, plant, and soul.​
 These inventions are limited in number due to the Sage’s ethical constraints: “Creation 
without care is conquest in disguise.” 
 

Varthum Chronosophic Instruments 
Varthum’s scientists built Temporal Modulation Devices derived from partial BE blueprints. 
Their primary function is stabilization of Ki-time flow during travel and anomaly research. 
Though mechanical in form, these instruments function closer to organs than machines, 
requiring resonance calibration with the user’s heartbeat.​
Misuse or untrained synchronization can create localized voids where time folds into itself. 
Mycomind Neural Interface 
Unlike mechanical engineering, the Mycomind evolved its own biotechnological paradigm. 
The Mycelium Neural Network (MNN) achieves distributed cognition through living tissue. 
Rather than replicating the BE legacy, it repurposes organic intelligence.​
Mycelial interfaces connect individuals across vast distances via biochemical signal transfer. 
While benign in intent, it concerns — when minds merge, what remains of the individual? 
“The Mycomind remembers what others forget — including the meaning of self.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 
12.3 The Kaerugan Paradigm Shift 
As Kaerugan moves toward independence, its engineers are developing Ferro-Ki 
composites—metals that can store, conduct, and emit Ki signatures. These materials later 
replace traditional relics and have immediate implications for intercontinental trade.​
 Such breakthroughs risk reigniting the BE recursion paradox: machines that can learn from 
organic input. However, the mechanist reformers envision a controlled synthesis — a 
“thinking metal” that adapts but never dreams. 
The Federation’s struggles show in its direction: self-forging and self-defining. 
 
12.4 On Artificial Sentience 
The philosophical divide between creation and life defines the post-Eclipse age. The 
question is no longer whether machines can think, but whether they should.​
Entities like Kodomaru and BE “dream engines” show that sentience is not confined to flesh, 
but consciousness, detached from mortality, tends toward recursion and collapse. 
Synthetic consciousness is thus treated not as advancement, but as a covenant of 
responsibility. Varthum scholars record failed attempts where memory replication led to 
entity self-dissolution — a condition known as Echo Death. 
“We learned that the soul is not information. It is interpretation.”​
 — Chronosophic Maxim, 7th Cycle

12.6 Closing Remarks 
The planet’s technologies, whether mechanical, organic, or dreamborne, all attempt to 
reconcile the same contradiction: to persist within time without mastering it.​
 The Infinitree’s symbiosis remains the ideal — a system that connects without 
consumption. Yet even that harmony now faces interference as mortals seek to graft its flow 
into machines. 
“The greatest invention remains restraint.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 

13.1 The Foundation: Ki and Manifestation 
All elemental systems within the Densetsu world are manifestations of Ki — the universal 
substrate of motion, emotion, and existence. Ki is not energy in the scientific sense, nor spirit 
in the religious; it is the living continuity between matter and intent, the intermediary 
through which the soul interacts with the world. 
When focused through will or technology, Ki polarizes into distinct frequencies, forming 
what sages call Concords — stable archetypes of known as the Nine Simple Elements. 
“Ki is the question the universe asks itself. The elements are its answers.”​
 — Bonsai Sage

13.3 Elemental Synthesis and Secondary Concords 
When two or more elemental frequencies overlap, Synthesis occurs, producing a Concord. 
These hybrid expressions are unpredictable and vary by wielder and intent. 
Examples include: 
●​ Steam (Fire + Water) — volatile and fast-moving; used in combustion turbines.​
 
●​ Dust (Earth + Wind) — kinetic barrier or blinding veil.​
 
●​ Lightning (Fire + Thunder) — refined form used in long-range combat channels.​
 
●​ Bloom (Gaea + Water) — healing Ki emission used in ritual gardens and Sage 
biotech.​
 
●​ Voidlight (Light + Dark) — paradoxical emission of perception without illumination, 
used in Chronosophic cloaking.​
 
●​ Chronofrost (Timespace + Water) — temperature reduction via local time dilation.​
 
The more abstract the synthesis, the closer it drifts toward temporal phenomena, risking 
instability or paradox. 
 
 

13.4 Concord Between Nature and Machine 
Not all elements welcome synthesis with artifice.​
 Each elemental archetype displays unique resistance or receptivity toward technological 
interference:

This explains why Kaerugan’s engineers favor Fire, Earth, and Thunder — their physical 
stability ensures industrial reliability — while Verdathal and the Sage’s constructs depend 
on Gaea and Water for sustainable growth. 
 
 

13.5 Elemental Resonance and Sentience 
Some individuals display elemental attunement, where Ki naturally aligns with one or more 
elements. This resonance shapes not only their abilities but their worldview.​
 Sages consider attunement to be a dialogue between soul and substrate — a reminder 
that mastery without empathy invites collapse. 
“To command the element is to share in its burden.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
Excess resonance results in Ki drift, a state where the wielder’s body or mind begins to 
embody elemental traits: stone-hard skin, perpetual heat, slowed or accelerated perception. 
Chronosophs classify these as Elemental Deviations, neither curse nor gift, but 
evolutionary responses to Ki overexposure. 
 
13.6 The Concord Network 
Across the continents, a natural equilibrium exists where elemental currents converge. This 
invisible structure, the Concord Network, underpins both magic and technology.​
 Temporal rifts, Ki wells, and leylines are merely visible distortions of this larger lattice. The 
Infinitree anchors the system — its roots and branches distribute Ki harmonically across the 
planet’s crust and atmosphere.​
 When its flow destabilizes, the Concord Network shifts, giving rise to elemental anomalies 
such as the Time-Broken Forests or Rust Blooms. 
 
13.7 Elemental Combat and Application 
Combat Ki control is both art and discipline.​
 Most practitioners use Catalysts — relics or biomechanical conduits that stabilize Ki 
discharge.​
 Techniques are categorized by their Flow Direction: 
●​ Internal Flow (Inward) — defensive or restorative, emphasizing control (e.g., Earth 
or Water stances).​
 
●​ External Flow (Outward) — offensive or expressive, emphasizing projection (e.g., 
Fire, Thunder).​
 
●​ Transversal Flow (Balanced) — adaptive; used by masters capable of redirecting Ki 
on contact (e.g., Wind, Gaea).​
 
High-tier manipulation of Timespace elements remains restricted to Chronosoph scholars 
and relic-users due to its potential for paradox. 

13.8 Closing Observations 
The study of elemental concord reveals that magic and science are parallel expressions 
of Ki literacy. One learns by meditation, the other by experiment; both risk imbalance 
through ignorance.​
 Technological and biological evolution now depend not on new elements, but on ethical 
alignment with Ki flow itself. 
“Power is not granted by element or relic — it is the echo of harmony or hubris.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 

14.1 The Principle of Circulation 
Ki does not flow uniformly across the world. It circulates through biological, geological, and 
atmospheric conduits, forming a living feedback system.​
 This phenomenon—called the Flow Ecology—defines every level of existence: from the 
heartbeat of a creature to the oscillation of weather fronts and tectonic plates. 
In natural balance, Ki moves in spiral trajectories, maintaining resonance between the 
Infinitree’s root network and the planet’s mantle.​
 When obstructed or consumed unevenly, these spirals collapse, producing localized rifts, 
corruption blooms, or still zones where time and matter lose coherence. 
“The Flow is not a current to be followed—it is the breath of the world inhaling 
itself.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 
14.2 The Flow Layers 
1.​ Subterranean Veins — Rootlike Ki conduits beneath the crust, sustaining mineral 
growth, fungal communication, and geothermal stability. The Mycomind Neural 
Network (MNN) occupies these depths, serving as both regulator and recorder. 
2.​ Surface Currents — The Ki that animates flora, fauna, and weather cycles. These 
are visible through environmental behavior—wind patterns shifting with emotion, 
rivers pulsing with bioluminescence. 
3.​ Atmospheric Drift — Diffuse Ki suspended in the air, interacting with celestial 
bodies. Light and Thunder elements dominate this layer, creating auroras and 
electrical storms that reflect global mood shifts. 
4.​ Astral Flow — The outermost band of circulating Ki, partially overlapping with 
Timespace fields. Dreaming Faith scholars believe the Astral Flow records intent, 
forming the substrate of prophetic vision. 
Each layer mirrors the others; damage in one tier ripples upward or downward, destabilizing 
entire regions. 
 
 

14.3 The Ecological Feedback Loop 
The Flow Ecology operates through absorption, transformation, and release: 
●​ Absorption — Lifeforms draw Ki from the environment through respiration, 
digestion, or photosynthesis. 
●​ Transformation — Organisms and machines convert Ki into movement, thought, 
and reaction. 
●​ Release — Upon death or decay, Ki returns, carrying new imprints of memory and 
experience.​
 
Disruption occurs when the release phase is obstructed—through hoarded relics, 
technological parasitism, or necrotic corruption.​
 The result is anomaly genesis: rifts where stagnant Ki attempts to recycle itself through 
unnatural means.

14.5 The Role of Creatures 
Creatures are Ki mediators, not anomalies by default.​
 Their life cycles mirror the flow phases—feeding, adapting, releasing. When the balance 
shifts, they mutate to fill ecological voids.​
 Corrupted creatures arise when Flow saturation exceeds biological tolerance, leading to 
parasitic overgrowth or temporal confusion. 
●​ Instinctual Creatures: Maintain natural equilibrium; their behavior often predicts 
shifts in local Ki pressure.​
 
●​ Corrupted Creatures: Manifest from stagnation; driven by residual memory rather 
than instinct.​
 
●​ Hybrid Entities: Occasionally form symbioses with relics or Mycomind spores, 
displaying partial sentience or altered moral alignment.​
 
These variations explain how combat and ecology remain intertwined—defeating a corrupted 
entity restores Ki flow, while excessive harvesting or relic use deepens imbalance. 
“Every monster is a message the world sends to itself.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 
14.6 Climatic and Temporal Phenomena 
Ki circulation defines weather as much as wind or heat. 
●​ Auroral Streams — Natural Ki release along atmospheric leylines; can affect dream 
patterns.​
 
●​ Temporal Drifts — Micro-rifts moving with storm fronts, briefly altering local time 
dilation.​
 
●​ Bloom Seasons — Periods of increased Gaea resonance when plants exhibit 
anomalous fertility or bioluminescence.​
 
●​ Rustfall Events — Metallic rainstorms from Kaerugan smog regions, where decayed 
Ki binds to particulate metal.​
 
Chronosophic institutes use these phenomena to predict regional crises, though prediction 
often arrives after disaster. 
 
 

14.7 Global Regulation — The Infinitree 
The Infinitree acts as the planet’s circulatory heart, distributing Ki across all strata.​
 Its roots penetrate the Subterranean Veins, and its canopy reaches into the Astral Flow. The 
Infinitree’s symbiosis with the planet maintains temporal balance; its rhythm aligns biological 
time with cosmic flow.​
 When anomalies disturb this equilibrium, the Infinitree compensates by rerouting flow 
through secondary nodes, such as deep rift zones or Mycomind fungal conduits.​
 This rerouting often causes localized surges—temporary bursts of abundance or ruin, 
misinterpreted by mortals as divine intervention. 
“The Infinitree dreams of balance, and the world responds in kind.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 
14.8 Ecological Consequences and Restoration 
Restoring balance requires realignment of the three Ki economies: 
1.​ Natural — Flora, fauna, and biotic exchange.​
 
2.​ Artificial — Technology, relics, and human extraction.​
 
3.​ Spiritual — Faith, ritual, and emotional resonance.​
 
Each contributes to flow continuity. Overemphasis on any one form collapses the others. 
Verdathal druids and Mycomind mediators use Reconciliation Rituals, which synchronize 
local Ki frequencies by merging organic and psychic pulses. 
In gameplay terms, these restoration acts manifest as area purification, reducing anomaly 
density while opening new ecological interactions (e.g., new species emergence or relic 
functionality). 
 
14.9 Closing Notes 
The Flow Ecology is the living text of the planet — every storm, bloom, and creature is a line 
in its ongoing dialogue.​
 Civilizations rise and fall within its breath. Understanding it is not mastery but participation; 
each act of violence, cultivation, or invention rewrites a fragment of the planet’s rhythm. 
“To live within the Flow is not to follow it, but to let it follow through you.”​
 — Bonsai Sage 
 

15.1 Biological Foundations 
The biosphere of Densetsu evolved under dual influence: Ki resonance and temporal 
stability. Flora and fauna developed separately, yet both exhibit Ki sensitivity, a reactive 
property allowing adaptation to Flow fluctuations.​
 Unlike mechanical systems, biology does not merely channel Ki—it interprets it. This 
interpretation grants resilience or mutation depending on balance. 
“Nature listens differently than man. Where we impose, it adjusts.”​
 — Bonsai Sage

These tiers form a continuum—transition from one to another reflects the health of the Flow

nodes. 
 
 

15.3 Fauna Diversity 
Mammalian and Anthropoid Species 
Found across temperate zones, they exhibit the highest degree of Ki reflexivity. Many 
evolved empathic senses that detect Flow changes long before anomalies occur.​
 Humans (Suhada) represent the most advanced of these—biologically ordinary but 
possessing unmatched adaptability to Ki frequencies. 
Avian Populations (Galportian Lineages) 
Ki adaptation manifests as magnetic and electro-sensory organs enabling navigation through 
Chronotides. Their hierarchy is deeply tied to species form—predators govern, couriers 
obey.​
 Post-republic Galport experiments have begun synthetic wing augmentation, blending 
Thunder Ki with mechanical stabilizers. 
Marine and Abyssal Species 
Deep-sea ecosystems thrive on Abyssal Ki, rich in resonance and decay potential.​
 The Marella people evolved through symbiosis with these zones, developing organs that 
filter corrupted Ki safely.​
 Other apex species, such as the Kuro-type cephalopods, retain ancestral memory traces 
dating to pre-Eclipse oceans. 
Fungal and Mycelial Lifeforms 
Constituting the biological extension of the Mycomind, these species merge sentience 
and nutrient cycles.​
 Mycelial blooms act as neural relays, while Spore Mimics function as 
emissaries—constructs of will and projection rather than body. 
Insectoid and Arthropodal Forms 
High mutation rate and Ki reactivity make them natural anomaly indicators.​
 One notable example is the Spirit Carapace Crab, a spectral crustacean found in the 
Vengeful Shores—its carapace infused with remnant sorrow. These organisms recycle 
emotional residue into matter, binding death and memory into ecological function. 
 
 

15.4 Flora and Fungal Systems 
Plants are primary Ki regulators, anchoring local frequencies. Many species evolved 
reflexive behaviors—root retraction during corruption surges, or seed dormancy in temporal 
loops. 
●​ Chronobloom: A time-sensitive flower used to measure flow irregularities. Petal 
rhythm indicates temporal strain; its presence marks active anomaly zones.​
 
●​ Gleamvine: Bioluminescent creeper from Eddara, reacts to approaching corruption 
by dimming.​
 
●​ Rustmoss: Found near Kaerugan foundries; thrives on metal and residual entropy, 
signaling industrial Ki distortion.​
 
●​ Verdant Towers: Colossal trees used as dwellings in Eddara, their structure guided 
by resonance singing.​
 
●​ Spore Lamps: Engineered fungal constructs from Mycomind, providing ambient 
illumination by sharing neural Ki light.​
 
Fungal species bridge plant and Mycomind biology, their roots interfacing with the 
Subterranean Veins that connect to the Infinitree’s lower strata. 
 
 

15.5 Environmental Anomalies 
Anomalies are world-scale physiological reactions—the planet’s immune responses to 
imbalance. They are not sentient, yet act with systemic intent, reshaping environments to 
restore flow through destruction or transformation.
